Issue #117 CLOSED. No economic claim, no speed claim |

| D-095 | V8.2 compute core S7: Verdict statistics + report/audit artifacts pass the parity gate. statistics/{mod,reality_check,detrended,remaining}.rs — four files, not the single statistics.rs COMPUTE_CORE_SPEC §6 named — implement the verdict subcommand (issue #128 wiring): block-bootstrap Reality-Check (reality_check.rs), the Appendix A detrended-null placebo invariant (detrended.rs, issue #124), and the METH-2..6 surface (remaining.rs, issue #129: permutation Reality-Check, bootstrap CI, effective independent episodes, regime slices, streak-vs-null, practical significance, expected false positives, effective search size). A new module not named in the original table, mt19937.rs (issue #127), reproduces CPython 3.14's Mersenne Twister bit-exactly — random.Random(int) seeding via init_by_array, random() via genrand_res53 (NOT getrandbits(53)/2**53, empirically different bits), and randrange/getrandbits/sample matching random.py's pool-branch Fisher-Yates with no k==n short-circuit — because the frozen oracle statistics.py draws every bootstrap/permutation resample from random.Random(seed) and bit-exact verdict parity requires bit-exact draws. report.rs (issue #126) is extended additively: verdict_path folds the verdict subcommand's JSON into the report summary (the report's own top-level claim stays NO_ECONOMIC_CLAIM, rule 12 — a p-value is not an authority receipt), and audit_report audits an existing artifact against the current tape/producer, the path by which the freshness check flags a stale artifact (issue #123) | PROVISIONAL_DECISION | D-032 file-family rule: the statistics/ directory split and mt19937.rs recorded in IMPLEMENTATION_LAYOUT §1.1/§4. Rule 12 held: verdict and report claims both stay NO_ECONOMIC_CLAIM. No economic claim, no speed claim | | D-096 | Kernel backend strategy: scalar reference + CPU/SIMD first, GPU gated behind the ~10^9-cell trigger, CubeCL preferred over wgpu if a portable layer is ever adopted. v8-core is single-path scalar today — the S0-S7 gates (D-087..D-095) are correctness gates; threads is recorded in the request manifest and tested for invariance (G5), but no task-parallel or SIMD execution exists in the tree (no std::thread/rayon/spawn, no target_feature, anywhere in v8-core/src/). The implementation order is declared: Backend-0 scalar deterministic Rust as the in-core reference — the frozen Python src/v8/ remains the parity oracle (D-087); Backend-0 is a within-core reference, not a second truth — then Backend-1 CPU + task parallelism + SIMD, and GPU only past the trigger (COMPUTE_SCHEDULING_SPEC §6, D-084). If a portable GPU layer is ever adopted it is CubeCL over wgpu: CubeCL compiles one kernel IR to CUDA/HIP/Metal/Vulkan/CPU and matches the K1-K6 kernel structure and the "backend is an implementation detail, never kernel semantics" rule (D-084), while wgpu sits at the buffer/bind-group/pipeline/dispatch level — direct wgpu use means writing a mini compute framework and owning the CUDA/HIP/Metal/Vulkan differences ourselves. No CubeCL/wgpu dependency is added now: CubeCL is alpha with breaking changes between minor versions, and O-029's precondition — the portable layer's CPU output measured competitive against a hand-written CPU kernel — precedes any adoption. K4 (replay/cube, no cross-worker reduction) is the only GPU candidate; K1/K2 reduction-order sensitivity (D-084) keeps them CPU-only | PROVISIONAL_DECISION | The f64 contract closes the GPU path before the trigger opens it. K4 requires f64 throughout + FMA contraction off + fast-math off, and GPU fp64 is a wall on every portable route: Metal exposes no fp64 in shaders; Vulkan exposes SHADER_F64 (native only) but fp64 runs 16-64x slower than f32 (wgpu feature docs); consumer CUDA/HIP (RTX 40/50, RX 7000) execute fp64 at 1/16-1/32 rate. On Apple, CubeCL's Metal/Vulkan paths run through wgpu, so the limitation applies either way. Consequences: (a) ~10^9 cells is a necessary but not sufficient condition — f64-on-GPU economics are doubtful on all consumer backends; (b) the only workload projected to reach the trigger, the Expert-variant sweep, was already brought below it by D-086's successive halving; (c) the backend-neutral interface boundary that keeps the ontology independent of the backend is cheap and worth having when Backend-1 lands, but the GPU backend itself is unlikely to ever be built. D-032 file-family rule: backend/ and scheduler.rs, when added, are recorded here and in the CHANGELOG; no such module exists in the tree today | | D-097 | The Rust compute plane is the authoritative implementation; the Python parity oracle is retired from the verification path (operator-ratified 2026-08-14). v8-core (the Rust plane, D-087..D-095) is the sole authoritative implementation. src/v8 — the frozen Python oracle (D-087) — is retired as the verification reference: it remains in the tree as a historical/legacy corpus but does not run in CI, in any workflow, or in any gate. Verification is Rust-native: cargo test --release per change (v8-core unit tests incl. the LEDGER_FORMAT_SPEC §8 battery and COMPUTE_SCHEDULING_SPEC §9 cheap tests), G4/G5 determinism (two runs byte-identical; threads=1 vs N byte-identical), and golden vectors pinned from the S0-S7-verified outputs as the release gate. The Python test suite (pytest tests, 864 tests incl. the 63 parity tests) is no longer a gate; tests/parity/ and the oracle-tree-hash pin mechanism (PARITY_AND_IDENTITY_SPEC §7.5) are superseded by golden-vector hashes | PROVISIONAL_DECISION | The retirement is only coherent because the differential verification it retires already completed: every S0-S7 gate (D-087..D-095, reports/parity/S0-S7.md) closed with the Rust plane byte-identical to a second, independently-implemented reference — that record is the provenance that licenses trusting Rust golden vectors going forward. Risk, stated honestly — self-verification circularity: Rust now verifies Rust; the independent reference is gone. Mitigation: golden vectors pin outputs that were independently cross-checked at S0-S7; a golden-hash change must be tied to a documented committed code change (no silent drift); the historical differential record stays in the tree as the trust anchor. Scope boundary, stated honestly: the rule covers the verification/execution path — no Python runs in CI, workflows, or gates. Repository tooling outside that path (the monograph build tools/build_monograph.py, tape builders, the vendored simtruth/ V7 lab whose authority is already NOT renewed) is unchanged for now; porting it is follow-up, not part of this decision. Rules 9 and 12 are unchanged: authority receipts now bind to Rust artifacts and their golden-vector hashes; the economic verdict remains NO_ECONOMIC_CLAIM. Defect fixes (issues #61-72 family) land in v8-core only; src/v8 is not edited further. CI: the two pytest steps in .github/workflows/ci.yml are removed; cargo + forbidden-name scan + monograph byte-identity probe remain |

| D-098 | Optional Linux Vulkan f64 K4 backend is implemented behind the backend boundary. v8-core/src/backend/gpu.rs provides a real wgpu Vulkan compute path for the static bar-close replay subset; it requires SHADER_F64, runs a no-contraction probe before adoption, validates geometry, and exposes CPU fallback for unavailable/unsupported devices. backend/mod.rs owns dispatch: Auto uses a runtime step threshold plus capability/fill-policy gates, explicit Gpu fails closed, and unsupported thesis/trigger/limit/management/pyramiding cells never enter the GPU kernel. Non-Linux builds report unavailable rather than pretending to dispatch. This supersedes D-096's implementation-status sentence (“backend itself is unlikely to ever be built”) without changing D-084's backend-invariance or f64 requirements. | PROVISIONAL_DECISION | Local evidence: default/debug/release builds, GPU-feature tests, strict Clippy, Linux-target check, and CPU/SIMD parity all pass; the Apple host correctly fails closed because it has no Vulkan shader-f64 runtime. A physical Linux Vulkan adapter with SHADER_F64 is still required to close the runtime GPU golden-parity receipt; no performance or economic claim is made. |

| D-099 | Computation is a budgeted decision, not a ritual. Before a computation expected to exceed five seconds, or an already-green check is repeated, state the decision it could change and the new semantic risk it resolves. Run it only when expected marginal decision value exceeds total cost (runtime, user latency, tool/context overhead, and redundancy). | PROVISIONAL_DECISION | Mandatory semantic-boundary gates still run: changed correctness semantics, determinism/parity/identity boundaries, safety/authority/data-integrity behavior, and explicit handoff gates. Prefer the smallest discriminating check; no suite rerun after documentation/format-only changes; full handoff suite at most once; extra post-green verification is limited to 60 seconds before reporting evidence and remaining uncertainty. See COMPUTATION_BUDGET_POLICY.md. |

| D-100 | Python oracle boundary is locked. src/v8/ is a frozen, hash-bound historical parity oracle; v8-core/ owns the request and verification path. Python may run only for explicitly named legacy research tooling, monograph generation, and boundary audits. A changed oracle tree requires a registry decision, changelog entry, and new hash; CI rejects unregistered edits and Python pytest/oracle invocation in the Rust gate. | LOCKED_INVARIANT candidate | docs/legacy/PYTHON_ORACLE_LOCK.json, docs/legacy/PYTHON_ORACLE_POLICY.md, and tools/audit_python_boundary.py. The oracle is retained because deleting it would remove the independent S0–S7 reference; this decision does not claim that every legacy Python tool has already been ported. |

Status vocabulary is intentional: LOCKED_INVARIANT constrains validity; PROVISIONAL_DECISION is reversible; REJECTED_OPTION means excluded from the named baseline, not disproven forever. None means profitability.

OPEN DECISIONS

V8 Open Decisions v0.1

ID Open question Cheapest resolving experiment Admission / rejection condition
O-024 Which literature preconditions are added as real filters, and which stay REJECTED_OPTION? (failed_breakout implements 2/10: close-back-in-range + invalidation; the other 8 — prior trend, exhaustion, sweep, rejection, volume, no-news, RR>2, structural stop — are absent, issue #65.) Per-condition feasibility: add each absent condition as a new variant (rule 12: a variant change is a challenger, never an in-place edit) and measure the conditional-population delta on the frozen OOS Retain a condition only if it survives the within-family Reality-Check and its search_universe_size cost is registered (D-046). Missing data (no news channel, no depth) is REJECTED_OPTION by data-availability; a design choice must be PROVISIONAL_DECISION and explicitly justified
O-025 What is the authoritative round_trip_cost_r? The shipped 0.07R (~4.5 bps on the dev window) is below a realistic taker round trip (8-10 bps); at an honest 0.125R the lab refuses to trade (6,209 excess_cost rejects, issue #61/#69). A venue-schema cost receipt: derive taker/maker fees + slippage from the Binance USDT-M fee schedule and a measured fill-vs-mid distribution, expressed in R at the declared barrier scale Adopt the derived number as the manifest default and re-run the dev-window feasibility measurement; the excess-cost gate then fires honestly instead of sitting just under the threshold
O-027 Is the 1R take-profit clipping a real favorable tail? The diagnostic engine's first real-tape run found 79% of target-exits continued >2R after exit (mean post-exit max +4.5R), and the intrabar ambiguity bracket is 1.79R wide (144 trades touched both barriers). If the favorable tail is signal, a wider TP / trailing-exit variant is a challenger (rule 12); if the tail is noise, the ambiguity tie-break is a classification artefact Two preregistered challengers on the frozen OOS, same entry set: (a) target_r ∈ {2, 3} and no-TP variants; (b) an optimistic-tie-break variant to measure the ambiguity bound Retain a variant only if it survives the within-family Reality-Check under D-046 multiplicity; the diagnostic finding itself is NOT evidence (authority NONE, O-025's cost receipt gates the whole direction)
O-002 What state representation adds value? raw window vs engineered state vs hybrid, equal data/model budget Retain only an incremental replicated result; learned latent state deferred.
O-003 Does full lifecycle improve learning/auditability? Candidate-transition table vs executed-only table for a fixed task Retain if it improves preregistered calibration/attribution; otherwise keep logging only if governance value justifies cost.
O-004 Is a router worthwhile? A self-gating vs deterministic pre-router paired replay Require near-perfect preregistered valuable-candidate recall plus binding resource gain.
O-005 Does a scorer add selection value? deterministic evidence score vs logistic vs shallow tree at matched coverage Require repeated OOS net-utility gain and calibration; fewer trades alone fails.
O-006 Is ranking necessary? all accepted candidates vs deterministic 1/N/risk cap during contention Require recurring contention and paired marginal portfolio utility gain.
O-007 Which fidelity is required? Sensitivity of the specific conclusion across Levels 0/1; advance only on material ambiguity If conclusion changes with unsupported fill assumptions, block it pending richer tape.
O-008 Can execution be specialized? fixed canonical execution under stress before any learned policy No learned executor without a certified positive Expert and stable canonical baseline.
O-009 Which storage engine for the derived/research store? The design mapping is resolved (D-031; PERSISTENCE_REPLAY_SPEC §1): immutable Parquet archive, JSONL append-only ledgers at slice scale, DuckDB derived/research tables from a pinned manifest. What stays open is scale validation only — adopt the DuckDB materializations if deterministic replay and hash audit pass at declared scale; otherwise keep the smallest engine (plain JSONL).
O-010 Is an event-driven (trade/depth) evaluation loop worth its cost? bar-driven vs event-driven on the same preregistered campaign Replace the bar driver only on replicated incremental utility or a declared fidelity defect.
O-011 Does the Binance adapter's coverage meet research needs? tape quality audit: gaps, funding schedule, depth coverage vs declared universe Extend venues only if the audit shows binding coverage/cost failure on the locked universe.
O-012 When do exposure conflicts justify a ranker? count + economics of EXISTING_EXPOSURE_CONFLICT rejections on the locked baseline Admit a ranker only when conflicts are frequent and economically material; 1/N or risk cap otherwise.
O-013 Does active position management (partial exit, breakeven, trail) beat static geometry? pessimistic-intrabar canonical comparison, costed Test only under the conservative same-bar rule; admission requires replicated OOS gain (dependent on O-007 resolution).
O-014 Do executed and portfolio-rejected populations differ systematically? two-sample KS + Mann-Whitney on executed vs portfolio-rejected counterfactual net_R Divergence does not license an OPE correction layer: deterministic admission gives rejected Candidates propensity 0 (deficient support), and no admissible finance OPE application was found. Divergence beyond the declared threshold blocks the economic verdict instead (D-027). What stays open is whether divergence is stable enough to characterise, not whether to correct for it.
O-015 Does a learned regime veto beat mechanical masks? learned regime label vs mechanical mask, same signal family Learnable filters enter through the router gate (EXPERT_PROTOCOL §3); mechanical masks are baseline.
O-016 Does drawdown-conditioned sizing beat fixed fractional risk? drawdown ladder vs fixed 1R, costed OOS Literature is empty for crypto perps; the program must produce its own evidence (Novy-Marx cautions on added degrees of freedom).
O-020 Should the history lookback window be declared per Expert instead of a global 32 bars? Add a declared history_bars to the Expert contract, build the state at max(declared) and let each Expert slice its own; compare ledger determinism and state-build cost against the current global constant D-034 pins 32 bars globally with a documented bound (anchors older than the window are unstable). That was sufficient for three pilots whose setups are short-horizon. With rule 14's Expert cap removed (D-043), it becomes a scaling constraint: a behavior family needing a longer lookback — weekly structure, slow regime — cannot be expressed at all, and the limit is invisible because nothing fails, the anchor just silently falls back to the window edge. Deliberately NOT implemented in the D-043 pass: it adds a public field to the Expert contract, which is a D-032 file-family change and needs its own register decision, and it moves the state hash.
O-019 Does the D-024 intrabar-range veto (max_bar_range_frac = 0.05) fire at all on the declared dev window, and is 0.05 a defensible level? Count TRADABILITY_MASK_VETO / BAR_RANGE firings on the declared 12-month BTCUSDT 1h dev tape (D-041) and report the (high-low)/close distribution with its upper quantiles D-036 defends 0.05 as "declared, never fitted", which answers the leakage objection but not the inertness one: a threshold no bar ever crosses is a veto that exists only on paper, and the corpus has never measured its firing rate. Fix the level pre-holdout by the O-017 method (derive on the dev window, fix before the frozen slice, never revisable after a verdict) or record that the veto is inert and remove it. Not a licence to fit: any replacement level is declared once, pre-holdout, and bound into the preregistration.
O-018 Should the portfolio heat cap scale with the Expert count, and on what basis? Sweep max_heat / max_cluster_heat against a fixed Expert population on the declared dev window; measure EXISTING_EXPOSURE_CONFLICT / PORTFOLIO_HEAT_EXCEEDED frequency and the D-027 attribution-validity diagnostics (execution_share, divergence KS) at each level Opened by D-043. With rule 14's Expert cap removed, max_heat = 3.0 (src/v8/risk.py) becomes the binding constraint on portfolio scale: adding Experts changes what is measured, not what is held. Raising it is not a configuration tweak — it changes concurrent risk and the executed population that every economic diagnostic reads, so it needs a preregistered comparison like any other component (rule 5). Admit a new level only on a costed frozen-OOS comparison against the current caps; a lower execution_share alone is not a reason to raise it. Caps stay at 3.0 / 2.0 until then.
O-021 Should the D-044 within-family Reality-Check test be pooled across families into one N-configuration statistic, replacing the top-level Bonferroni too? Build a bar-level (not episode-level) panel: for every bar in the frozen OOS window, each evaluated configuration across all families contributes its episode net_R if one is anchored/live at that bar, else 0; run reality_check_p_value-style resampling over that panel instead of per-family episode series Opened by D-044. Within a family, all variants fire on the same setup predicate, so episode grids are already aligned and src/v8/statistics.reality_check_p_value applies directly. Across families the grids are disjoint (different episode_key sets, different bar counts), so the same function cannot be called on the union without either misaligning series or fabricating a shared index. White's (2000) original construction assumes a common time index for all N rules, which a bar-level zero-filled panel would restore — but that changes what "no signal" means for a family that fires rarely (mostly zeros) versus one that fires often, and needs its own validity argument before being trusted. Admit pooling only after that argument is made and tested on synthetic data with a known ground truth; until then the two-level structure (Bonferroni across families, Reality-Check within a family) stays the interim design and is not merely conservative — it is the version actually verified.
O-022 Family results are NOT independent under rule 16's one-exposure-per-(instrument, direction) gate: a family's SHORT episodes are crowded out while another family's SHORT position is open, so the executed populations are coupled. Does this bias the family-level verdicts, and how should it be handled? Measure cross-family admission blocking on the dev window: for each REJECTED EXISTING_EXPOSURE_CONFLICT, record which family/direction held the blocking slot; report the blocking matrix and how each family's executed n and mu_hat change when the blocker is removed from the population (the failed_breakout -> liquidity_sweep flip, n 55 -> 73 and mu_hat +0.111 -> -0.086 when failed_breakout's spurious SHORTs were fixed, is the concrete observed case) Opened by the 2026-08-06 audit. Measured on the corrected dev diagnostic (2026-08-06, D-052 code): of 11,673 EXISTING_EXPOSURE_CONFLICT rejections, ALL had a same-direction open slot at the block time; bollinger_breakout alone blocked 4,115 candidates (incl. 1,226 of its own — self-blocking: the family's own open positions suppress its re-entries). Per-family execution_share = n_executed / (n_executed + n_portfolio_rejected) (the D-027 definition): only 6 of 21 families clear the 0.25 floor (bollinger_breakout 0.273, bollinger_reversion 0.567, trend_pullback 0.313, liquidity_sweep 0.308, fib_projection 0.350, divergence 0.400); 15 sit below, five of them under 0.10 (pandf 0.004, market_profile 0.065, trend_pullback_depth 0.098, obv_adl 0.104, macd 0.116). On the current slate the D-027 attribution-validity gate would score only ~6 families on the OOS and mark the rest ATTRIBUTION_UNSAFE_*. Flood ranking (candidates x blocks-caused): bollinger_breakout 14.97 (but 0.273 share — a legitimate family), volume_climax 11.05, market_profile 6.16, floor_trader_pivot 2.71. De-crowding response (2026-08-07): the disciplined lever is GATE SELECTIVITY, not retirement — retiring a family on its dev-window execution_share is selection bias (the dev window decides nothing). volume_climax gained the D-055 strict-climax challenger (3-sigma) so the flood tightens only if it survives the frozen-OOS Reality-Check; the other flooders get the same treatment if a declared selectivity challenger exists (market_profile's variant-d distance gate is the natural lever), otherwise their low share stays visible as ATTRIBUTION_UNSAFE on the OOS rather than being hidden by deletion. Two readings remain: (a) the coupling is an honest feature of a single-portfolio evaluation and the per-family verdicts are conditional on the OTHER families being present — document it as such and keep the per-family tests; (b) the coupling corrupts the per-family multiplicity accounting (each family's search was implicitly conditioned on the portfolio's exposure history), so a family-level verdict needs either a de-coupled evaluation (one family per run, at the cost of portfolio realism) or a bar-level panel that models the exposure contention (see O-021). Until a preregistered comparison exists, record the blocking matrix alongside every dev/OOS report and do not read a family's verdict as if it were measured in isolation.
O-023 The 2026-08-06 bug fixes changed the behavior of registered families (failed_breakout two-step gate, fib origin-based extensions, climax-bar anchor): are these v1 bug-completions (the code now matches its own frozen hypothesis) or v2 challengers (new behavior needing a fresh registry decision + multiplicity accounting)? Compare each fixed family's old vs new episode series on the frozen dev window; if the fixed series is a strict subset of the old (fewer, hypothesis-conforming episodes — failed_breakout n 369 -> 76), treat as bug-completion and keep v1; if the series is NOT a subset (new episodes the old gate could not produce — volume_climax 67 -> 380, fib_projection 2 -> 7), the behavior is new and needs a v2 entry Bug-completions keep v1 and their registered search_universe_size/variants_evaluated (the search never contained the buggy behavior as a deliberate configuration). Families whose fix ADDS episodes a v1 gate could not have produced are challengers: register a v2 variant (or a new family) with its own D-044/D-046 accounting before any frozen OOS uses it, per "versions evolve by challenger + frozen-OOS + registry decision, never in place" (rule 15, D-023). The frozen OOS must run on the version the preregistration names.
O-026 Should the locked universe extend beyond Binance USD-M crypto perps to equities (NASDAQ, BIST) — i.e. is a behavior family's hypothesis asset-class independent, or perp-specific? This is a NEW research axis, not a venue extension: equities have no funding/mark/index/premium tapes, a session calendar with gaps and corporate actions, and a different cost/authority structure, so it is NOT covered by O-011's "extend venues only on binding coverage failure" The cheapest resolving experiment is sequenced AFTER the base case, by rule 12: run v8_slice_001 (Phase-4 gate) on the locked perp universe first; only a family that survives there may be tested cross-asset. Then replay that family's EXACT frozen Expert geometry (same expert_version, same variants, same thresholds) on an equities tape built to the same three-clock PIT discipline, with a session-aware bar clock and a venue-appropriate cost model, as a preregistered replication with its own D-044/D-046 multiplicity accounting. Data-plane work may proceed in parallel as research-only (rule 11 "explore broadly in development"; rule 9 hash-binding) without touching the decision path Admit equities into the locked universe only if (a) a family survives the Phase-4 base-case gate on the perp universe AND (b) the same frozen family replicates after-cost signal on the equity venue under its own multiplicity controls. A failure to replicate is a valid falsification result — it says the hypothesis is perp-specific, which is informative, not a defect. Any new equities tape/builder is a D-032 file-family decision (new src/v8/ or tools/ module) and must meet rule 9's source-authority binding first — a hard constraint confirmed by the 2026-08-07 source survey: no source, free OR paid, offers the checksum-verifiable immutable archive rule 9 requires. Closest candidates — NASDAQ: Massive (ex-Polygon) Flat Files (official SIP daily files since 2003, bulk + immutable, but paid ≥ $29/mo and no published checksums — only S3 ETags) or stooq bulk ZIPs (checksumless); BIST: official DataStore (paid, contractually "Confidential", no checksum, no accuracy guarantee) or the free İş Yatırım API (unofficial, mutable, ~1997-present, violates every rule-9 axis). Until a source with a verifiable checksum binding appears, equities exist only as a documented research direction (D-065), never as a canonical dataset

Resolved

ID Resolution Evidence / condition
O-017 Attribution-validity thresholds ratified by the operator on 2026-08-01: execution_share floor = 0.25; population-divergence threshold = two-sample KS on net_R (executed vs portfolio-rejected) ≤ 0.20 Derived pre-holdout from the session-2 development baseline (execution_share 0.4156, KS 0.073; v8-dev-2026q2-btcusdt), bound into PREREGISTRATION_V8_SLICE_001 §15. The gate itself is D-027; the numbers are fixed before the frozen slice and never revisable after a verdict.

| O-028 | ~~Is the Expert-variant sweep dimension inside V8.2's scope?~~ RESOLVED 2026-08-11 by D-086: sweeps are admitted, under SWEEP_PROTOCOL. The premise of the question — that a sweep multiplies cells by its full cardinality — does not hold once compute is allocated by successive halving: the cost falls one to two orders of magnitude, landing back below the ~10^9-cell GPU trigger. Consequently sweeps do not force Experts native from stage S1 and do not reopen the GPU question | — | — | | O-029 | Which GPU backend, if the D-084 trigger ever fires — vendor-specific (HIP / Metal) or a backend-portable kernel layer? | Before adopting any portable layer, measure its CPU output against a directly-written CPU kernel on K4; the portable form is only free if its CPU path is competitive | Adopt a portable layer only if its CPU backend is within a declared margin of hand-written Rust; otherwise write CPU kernels directly and defer GPU entirely. Either way the choice must not become visible in a manifest or a hash (D-084). Annotated by D-096 (2026-08-14): CubeCL is the named candidate if the portable path is ever adopted, and the f64 contract makes the path economically doubtful on every consumer backend (Metal: no fp64 in shaders; Vulkan SHADER_F64 native-only at 16-64x slower than f32; consumer CUDA/HIP fp64 at 1/16-1/32 rate) — the question may resolve as "never" without ever reaching the CPU-competitiveness measurement | | O-030 | When does the Expert evaluate() layer port to the compute plane (stage S4)? The measured churn argues for late: src/v8/experts/ was touched by 51 of the last 200 commits — ~2.5x the next-most-churned module (lab.py, 20) — and docs/ by 146, so the specification itself is still moving | Re-measure directory churn at each stage gate; port when the expert and docs churn rates fall to the level of the already-frozen modules (simulator.py 10, statistics.py 2, tools/regret.py 1) | Port S4 early only if O-028 resolves "sweep in scope". Otherwise porting a moving specification makes the parity oracle fail for legitimate reasons and destroys the ability to distinguish a port defect from a specification change | | O-031 | Should docs/tr/ reach parity with the EN corpus? It is currently a subset — ARCHITECTURE_SPEC, IMPLEMENTATION_LAYOUT, CHANGELOG, ROADMAP, AGENT_RUNBOOK and the V8.2 contracts have no TR counterpart | Count reader demand for site/tr.html sections that do not exist, rather than translating speculatively | Translate a document only when a reader needs it; a stale translation is worse than an absent one, because the monograph presents both as current |

| O-032 | Which e-process construction is valid for V8's block-dependent episode streams? D-086 admits sweeps but blocks on this: the existing machinery (select_block_size, effective_independent_episodes, bootstrap_ci) estimates dependence with fixed-sample devices, and an anytime-valid analogue is required for the e-BH guarantee to mean anything here | Construct a betting-style test martingale on block-aggregated episodes, then calibrate it on a detrended / label-shuffled tape where no variant has an edge and check the realized FDR against the declared level over repeated campaigns (SWEEP_PROTOCOL §7.1) | Admit a construction only if it holds its declared level under the null calibration AND retains usable power against a planted effect. Until then no sweep campaign may run — D-086 is admitted but blocked, not live |

All remaining rows are OPEN_QUESTION. No field may be silently resolved by a dashboard result, unregistered parameter sweep, or synthetic data.
